# Layout review of the method proof design

This is the review record of the `method-proof` board, a throwaway synthetic design that is not for manufacture, kept as `docs/board-design-method.md` describes in its Layout review section. Each round was made by a reviewer that did not design the board, from the board's design files, the catalogue, the rendered views, the pre-order check's report and the committed layout plan.

## Round 1

**Board judged**: 9c2e5267504d91518c139326ae915329ef8f29c2

**Views**: `task hw:views -- --board method-proof.kicad_pcb --project method-proof.kicad_pro --class Power --class Supply --class Ground --out /Users/david/.claude/method-proof-work/r1/method-proof-views` and `task hw:placement -- --board method-proof.kicad_pcb --project method-proof.kicad_pro --out /Users/david/.claude/method-proof-work/r1/method-proof-placement`

**Pre-order check**: the board's DESIGN line and the FIGURE lines under it, from `/Users/david/.claude/method-proof-work/r1/preorder-report.txt`, no VIOLATION line:

```
DESIGN | hardware/proof/method-proof/method-proof.kicad_pro | PASS
FIGURE | hardware/proof/method-proof/method-proof.kicad_pro | WORK-2 route length | WORK_DETOUR_RATIO | 1.3 times the shortest straight-line tree joining the net's pads
FIGURE | hardware/proof/method-proof/method-proof.kicad_pro | WORK-4 branch at a pad | WORK_PAD_ANGLE_DEG | 90 degrees at least between two of three or more tracks of a net that leave a pad on one layer
FIGURE | hardware/proof/method-proof/method-proof.kicad_pro | WORK-8 narrowing | WORK_NECK_MM | 1 mm off its net's pads that a track narrower than its net class's track width may run
FIGURE | hardware/proof/method-proof/method-proof.kicad_pro | WORK-9 direction | WORK_ANGLE_TOLERANCE_DEG | 0.1 degrees off the board's edges or 45 degrees to them, and off a long pad's axis
FIGURE | hardware/proof/method-proof/method-proof.kicad_pro | WORK-10 pad entry | WORK_PAD_ENTRY_RATIO | 2 times as long as wide, beyond which a pad is entered only along its long axis
FIGURE | hardware/proof/method-proof/method-proof.kicad_pro | MAINS-5 barrier copper | BARRIER_COPPER_MM2 | 0 mm² of copper at most between the pad rows of a part that bridges the barrier
FIGURE | hardware/proof/method-proof/method-proof.kicad_pro | MFG-4 paste over a via | PASTE_OVER_VIA_MM2 | 0 mm² at most of a via's hole under a paste opening
FIGURE | hardware/proof/method-proof/method-proof.kicad_pro | MFG-6 pour gap | Board Setup min_clearance | 0.09 mm, the narrowest gap a pour may leave between its fill and copper of its own net that it stops short of
FIGURE | hardware/proof/method-proof/method-proof.kicad_pro | WORK-4 corner at a pad | the rules file's track_angle | 135 degrees at least between two tracks of a net that meet at a pad, as at any other joint
```

### Plan and free pass

**Regions.** The floorplan's two regions and their parts are what the layout holds. The low voltage region is (100, 100) to (119, 130) and the digital region (119, 100) to (140, 130); the board carries one rule area for each, on F.Cu, B.Cu, In1.Cu and In2.Cu, at exactly those bounds (every layer view draws them), and none for the mains and analog sensing regions, which the plan records as none. `measures.txt` lists the same parts per region as the plan, with OUTSIDE 0, and the courtyards read off the fabrication outlines lie inside their regions, JP2's to x 118.77 and C3's from x 119.22 being the nearest to the line. `layout-plan.md` holds no board's own items (one row, none), so there is nothing further to judge there.

**Connectors.** The edges are met: J1 and J2 stand at the left edge (bodies from x 100.6) and J3 at the right edge (body to x 138.3, 1.7 mm inside the outline); JP1 and JP2 are link positions with no edge, as the table says. The positions, read as the table defines them, the board coordinates of pin 1, are not all met. J1's pin 1 is at (104, 103.5) against (103, 104), 1.1 mm off; J2's at (104, 113) against (103, 114), 1.4 mm; J3's at (137, 114.775) against (137, 112), 2.8 mm; JP1's at (113, 103.5) against (110, 104), 3.0 mm; JP2's at (117, 114.5) against (113, 122), 8.5 mm. The method sets no tolerance for a position; I took J1 and J2 as met and J3, JP1 and JP2, which are off by more than one 2.54 mm pin pitch, as departures: finding 1.7.

**The yes rows of the plan table.** FLOOR-1, FLOOR-3, FLOOR-4, POWER-1, TEST-1, TEST-2, MFG-1, MFG-4, MFG-5, MFG-6, EMI-1, EMI-2, EMI-5 and WORK-2 to WORK-10 are met as their rows say; the numbers each row gives were measured against the board's files and fabrication outlines (U1 12 mm from J1 and U2 9.8 mm from U1, C3 2.9 mm and C2 3.0 mm from their pins, the test points 2.15 mm or more from the tall parts, R1 5.997 mm from U2 pin 11). POWER-2's row is met, its pours and tracks running as the row names them. CONN-3's row says the connectors face off the board; J1, J2 and J3 are top-entry parts, so the layout meets the row only in the sense that their cables leave away from U2 (noted at CONN-3). CONN-4's holes are where the row puts them (H1 (104, 126.5), H2 (137, 102.5), H3 (137, 127.5)); I did not find J1, the farthest connector from any hole (23 mm from H1), a departure, since neither the row nor the item says how near a hole must be. WORK-1's row says no connection crosses another on F.Cu, and none does. The rows not met are these. GROUND-2 says no opening in the planes is a merged one: the thermal-relief ring of J1 pin 2 and of J2 pin 3 merges with the clearance ring of the neighbouring pin on In1.Cu and In2.Cu (finding 1.1). DECOUPLE-1 says no via between a capacitor and its pin: the +5V via at (113.6, 109.5) is one (finding 1.2, also the item's fail). DECOUPLE-2 says two vias beside each capacitor's ground pad and C1's and C2's ground sides at U1 pin 1: each has one via, and the ground pads are 5.5 mm and 8.0 mm from pin 1 (finding 1.3). GROUND-1 says each ground pad reaches the planes through its own vias: U1 pin 1 has none within 3.9 mm (finding 1.4). POWER-3 says the power paths run on F.Cu end to end, changing layer only to the planes: +5V runs 31 mm on B.Cu to R1 (finding 1.5). EMI-3 says two vias for each of C5 and C4: one via is shared (finding 1.6).

**Free pass.** I looked at every tile of every view, enlarging tiles of the existing images where a feature was small (no new render; the crops are in the reviewer's scratch folder only), and ran KiCad's own rule check, `kicad-cli pcb drc --severity-all --all-track-errors`, on a scratch copy of the design files outside the repository: 0 violations, 0 unconnected items, 0 footprint errors. Beyond the findings above I saw the following and judged it not a defect. The silkscreen, paste, mask, Edge.Cuts and Cmts.User views each show a filled disc at every through hole and mounting hole and at no surface-mount pad, the same discs on Edge.Cuts, which carries no such shape, so they are the renderer drawing the drills and not silkscreen or paste. The +3V3 pour reaches U2 pin 4 by a finger 0.45 mm wide and pin 9 by one 0.35 mm wide, narrower than the 0.5 mm Power class width but not below the 0.3 mm Supply class width, entering the pads along their long axes. U2 pin 7's ground track ends in a via under the package outline, tented (F.Mask shows no opening there). Mounting holes and test points have no 3D model by design. The R1 feed on B.Cu hugs x 118.66, 0.34 mm inside the digital region's edge, in the low voltage region, and crosses no other net's copper. The silkscreen's lack of reference designators and the overlapping designators on the fabrication layers are finding 1.8.

### Plan and free pass

**Regions.** The floorplan's two regions and their parts are what the layout holds. The low voltage region is (100, 100) to (119, 130) and the digital region (119, 100) to (140, 130); the board carries one rule area for each, on F.Cu, B.Cu, In1.Cu and In2.Cu, at exactly those bounds (every layer view draws them), and none for the mains and analog sensing regions, which the plan records as none. `measures.txt` lists the same parts per region as the plan, with OUTSIDE 0; the courtyards on `assembly-top-r1c1.png` lie inside their regions, JP2's to x 118.8 and C3's from x 119.2 being the nearest to the line. `layout-plan.md` holds no board's own items (one row, none), so there is nothing further to judge there.

**Connectors.** The edges are met: J1 and J2 stand at the left edge (bodies from x 100.6) and J3 at the right edge (body to x 138.3); JP1 and JP2 are link positions with no edge. Pin 1 positions against the table: J1 (104, 103.5) against (103, 104), 1.1 mm; J2 (104, 113) against (103, 114), 1.4 mm; J3 (137, 114.775) against (137, 112), 2.78 mm; JP1 (113, 103.5) against (110, 104), 3.04 mm; JP2 (117, 114.5) against (113, 122), 8.50 mm. FLOOR-5's 2.54 mm leaves J1 and J2 met and J3, JP1 and JP2 departures. JP1's is finding 1.7, whose reason (TEST-1) I re-checked and which still applies: with TP1 fixed at (109, 105), a JP1 anywhere within 2.54 mm of the table position stands at most 1.76 mm from TP1's pad, short of the 2.0 mm TEST-1 asks of a part taller than 2.6 mm. The reasons finding 1.7 gave for J3 (WORK-2 and WORK-10) and JP2 (TEST-1) do not hold when tested the same way, so both are raised anew as finding 2.1.

**The yes rows of the plan table.** FLOOR-1, FLOOR-3, FLOOR-4, POWER-1, POWER-2, TEST-1, TEST-2, MFG-1, MFG-4, MFG-5, MFG-6, EMI-1, EMI-2, EMI-3, EMI-5, CONN-3, CONN-4, GROUND-1, DECOUPLE-1 and WORK-1 to WORK-10 are met as their rows say, the numbers each row gives measured against the board's files and the fabrication outlines (U1 13 mm from J1 and U2 9.8 mm from U1, C3 2.9 mm and C2 3.0 mm from their pins, TP1 to TP4 at least 2.15 mm from the taller parts, R1 5.997 mm from U2 pin 11, H1 (104, 126.5), H2 (137, 102.5), H3 (137, 127.5) against the row's near (103.5, 126), (137, 102.5), (137, 127.5)). The CONN-3 row's J1 and J2 about 25 mm from U2 is 23.6 mm and 21.8 mm centre to centre and 17 mm body to body; the row says about, and what it serves, a cable that leaves away from U2 and never folds over it, is met. The rows not met are these. POWER-3 says the power paths run on F.Cu end to end and change layer only to the planes: +5V runs 32.4 mm on B.Cu to R1 (finding 2.2; round 1's finding 1.5 recorded it and was closed by a reason that does not hold on the board as it stands, as the finding sets out, so it is raised anew rather than cited). DECOUPLE-2 says C1's and C2's ground sides are at U1 pin 1: they are 5.4 mm and 8.0 mm from it (finding 2.3). DECOUPLE-2's two vias of their own beside each capacitor's ground pad, GROUND-1's vias at each ground pad, DECOUPLE-1's no via between a capacitor and its pin, GROUND-2's no merged ring and EMI-3's two vias each for C5 and C4, the departures of findings 1.1 to 1.4 and 1.6, are now met (U1 pin 1 has two vias of its own, C1, C2 and C3 two each, the C5 and C4 ground pads two or more within 1.6 mm, the J1 and J2 pin rings 0.35 mm apart on both planes, the +5V tap via outside C1 and U1 pin 3's span).

**Free pass.** I looked at every tile of every view, enlarging parts of the existing tiles where a feature was small (the enlargements are crops of the rendered images, made in `/Users/david/.claude/method-proof-work/r2-reviewer-crops`, no new render). Beyond the findings above I saw the following and judged it not a defect. The silkscreen, paste, mask, Edge.Cuts, Cmts.User and fabrication views each show a filled disc at every through hole and mounting hole and at no surface-mount pad, the renderer drawing the drills. On both B.Cu tiles the +5V run at x 118.66 and the rings of JP2's pins leave a keyhole opening in the B.Cu ground pour, 0.55 mm wide, where a strip of pour only 0.2 mm wide would have been left between the pad's and the track's clearances: KiCad leaves no sliver there, the opening is within every clearance, and the pour runs on both sides of the track. U1's designator stands 0.2 mm from the pin 1 mark on `layer-F.SilkS-r1c1.png`, clear by the silkscreen minimum (no report violation). C5's designator at (110.95, 114.3) stands 2.6 mm from C5 and 2.55 mm from C4, between them, while C4's own designator stands below C4; I read it as C5's from the pattern of the others and did not raise it. On `layer-F.Fab-r1c1.png` and `layer-F.Fab-r2c1.png` the designators of JP1, JP2 and J3 lie over their own pads, on a fabrication layer that is not built. The 3D views draw J1, J2, J3, JP1 and JP2 as top-entry parts with cables leaving upward, and the mounting holes, test points and J3 stand where the layer views put them. The one thing wrong that no item asks about is the reading direction of the silkscreen text: the designators read in four directions, U1's upside down, and the test point names on the back read upside down when the board is turned over side to side (finding 2.4). Every track in the layer views runs along the outline or at 45 degrees to it.

### Findings

| Finding | Raised by | Severity | What and where | Rule | Added as | Planning shortfall | Closed by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.1 | FLOOR-5 | cosmetic | Pin 1 of J3 is at (137, 114.775) against the table's (137, 112), 2.78 mm, and of JP2 at (117, 114.5) against (113, 122), 8.50 mm, both over the 2.54 mm FLOOR-5 allows; seen on `assembly-top-r1c1.png`, `assembly-top-r2c1.png`, `placement-r1c1.png` and `placement-r2c1.png`. The edges are met. Finding 1.7 closed both by reasons that do not hold on the board as it stands. For JP2 the reason was TEST-1, but with TP2 at (112.5, 118) a JP2 at the table position would have its body (x 111.73 to 114.27, y 120.73 to 125.81) 2.23 mm from TP2's pad edge, over the 2.0 mm TEST-1 asks, so TEST-1 does not force JP2 off its position, and the plan's placement record names no other placement item that moved it. For J3 the reasons were WORK-2 and WORK-10, but neither conflicts with a position within tolerance: J3 at y 114.04 or less, within 2.54 mm of the table, takes a route to U2 pin 12 with one 45-degree jog whose segments are over the 1 mm WORK-5 asks, entering the pad along its axis and well under WORK-2's 1.3 times. JP1's departure (3.04 mm) stays under finding 1.7, which still applies. | no: a position in the plan's markdown table is not a figure the board's files, its rules or its variables hold, so no check can read it | FLOOR-5, which already states it | — | change: 60daedd, J3 moved to its table position (137, 112) with PA1_HDR drawn to it; overridden by: POWER-2 and FLOOR-3 for JP2, whose plan rows place the link in series between U1's output and the rail, beside U1 and U2, which a position at the table's (113, 122), 7 mm from U1 and off the supply path, would break; and TEST-1 for JP1, as finding 1.7 recorded |
| 2.2 | plan, row POWER-3 | function | The row says the power paths run on F.Cu end to end and change layer only to the planes. On `net-+5V-r1c1.png` and `net-+5V-r2c1.png`, +5V leaves F.Cu by a via at (113, 107.9), runs 32.4 mm on B.Cu ((116.06, 107.9), (118.66, 110.5), down x 118.66 to y 122.9, (119.66, 123.9), east to x 131.5) and returns by a via at (131.5, 123.9) to R1's pad; it carries R1's 5 mA only. The item POWER-3 itself holds, one via at each end for 5 mA. Finding 1.5 recorded the same departure and was closed as overridden by GROUND-1 and DECOUPLE-6; that reason does not hold now: GROUND-1 is held by the solid In1.Cu and In2.Cu planes whether or not an F.Cu run cut the F.Cu pour, and DECOUPLE-6 restricts vias in the span of a capacitor and its pin, not tracks, and the tap via stands outside that span. What remains true is that an F.Cu run cannot reach R1 without crossing the +3V3 and /+3V3_REG pours and J2 pin 2's +3V3 connection, which is the reason a closure has to name. | no: it is this board's own statement about which layer a path takes, not a practice, and whether a layer change is needed is judgement on the views | POWER-3, which already states layer changes of a supply path | — | overridden by: POWER-2, whose wide F.Cu pours for +3V3 and /+3V3_REG, with J2 pin 2's +3V3 connection, wall F.Cu off between the +5V pour and R1, so the 5 mA feed crosses on B.Cu with one via at each end, as POWER-3 allows for 5 mA |

Finding 1.3 recorded these distances with the single vias and was closed by 0e241be, which added the second vias only; the distances are unchanged, so the clause is still unmet. The item DECOUPLE-2 passes: two vias of their own each and the planes beneath. | no: where a capacitor's ground side stands relative to a pin is a placement judgement, DECOUPLE-2 judges the loop on the crop, and no figure for the distance has a source here | DECOUPLE-2, which already states it | — | overridden by: DECOUPLE-2, which C1 and C2 meet by two vias of their own beside each ground pad and the F.Cu ground pour that joins their ground pads, U1 pin 1 and the planes; the plan row's pin 1 was one way of meeting it |

### Plan and free pass

**Regions.** The floorplan's two regions and their parts are what the layout holds. The low voltage region is (100, 100) to (119, 130) and the digital region (119, 100) to (140, 130); the board carries one rule area for each, on F.Cu, B.Cu, In1.Cu and In2.Cu, at exactly those bounds (every layer view draws them as dashed hatched outlines), and none for the mains and analog sensing regions, which the plan records as none. `measures.txt` lists the same parts per region as the plan, with OUTSIDE 0 (LENGTH 144.1293 mm, CROSSINGS 11); on `layer-F.CrtYd-r1c1.png` and `layer-F.CrtYd-r2c1.png` every courtyard lies inside its region, JP2's to x 118.8 and C3's from x 119.2 being the nearest to the line. `layout-plan.md` holds no board's own items (one row, none), so there is nothing further to judge there.

**Connectors.** The edges are met: J1 and J2 stand at the left edge (bodies from x 100.6) and J3 at the right edge (body to x 138.3); JP1 and JP2 are link positions with no edge. Pin 1 positions against the table: J1 (104, 103.5) against (103, 104), 1.12 mm; J2 (104, 113) against (103, 114), 1.41 mm; J3 (137, 112) against (137, 112), 0 mm, its departure of finding 2.1 now removed with PA1_HDR drawn to it; JP1 (113, 103.5) against (110, 104), 3.04 mm; JP2 (117, 114.5) against (113, 122), 8.50 mm. FLOOR-5's 2.54 mm leaves J1, J2 and J3 met and JP1 and JP2 departures, both already raised and closed by a reason, which I re-tested against the board as it now stands rather than took on trust. JP1 (finding 1.7, TEST-1): TP1 stands at (109, 105) and the plan's placement record names candidate 5, which moved JP1 3 mm right so that TEST-1 passes; at the table position the pad of TP1 would lie inside JP1's body, and the nearest a JP1 within 2.54 mm of the table position can stand to TP1's pad is 1.76 mm, short of the 2.0 mm TEST-1 asks of a part taller than 2.6 mm, so the closing reason still applies, and FLOOR-5 itself says a placement item that moved a connector closes the departure. JP2 (finding 2.1, POWER-2): the plan's POWER-2 row puts JP2 in series between U1's output and the logic rail, beside U1 and U2; /+3V3_REG now runs U1 pin 2 to JP2 pin 1 and +3V3 JP2 pin 2 to U2 pin 9, about 15 mm of supply path in all, where JP2 at (113, 122) would make it about 30 mm, a detour POWER-2's short and direct path rules out, and no place within 2.54 mm of (113, 122) is any nearer, so that reason also still applies. Neither is raised again.

**The yes rows of the plan table.** FLOOR-1, FLOOR-3, FLOOR-4, POWER-1, POWER-2, GROUND-1, GROUND-2, DECOUPLE-1, TEST-1, TEST-2, MFG-1, MFG-4, MFG-5, MFG-6, EMI-1, EMI-2, EMI-3, EMI-5, CONN-3, CONN-4 and WORK-1 to WORK-10 are met as their rows say, the numbers each row gives measured against the board's files and the fabrication outlines (U1 13.4 mm from J1 and U2 9.8 mm from U1, C3 2.9 mm and C2 3.0 mm from their pins, TP1 to TP4 at least 2.1 mm from the taller parts, R1 5.997 mm and D1 3.4 mm from U2 pin 11, H1 (104, 126.5), H2 (137, 102.5), H3 (137, 127.5) against the row's near (103.5, 126), (137, 102.5), (137, 127.5), the PA1_HDR route 9.49 mm against a 8.78 mm straight tree, 1.08 times). The CONN-3 row's J1 and J2 about 25 mm from U2 is 23.6 mm and 21.8 mm centre to centre and 17 mm body to body; the row says about, and what it serves, a cable that leaves away from U2 and never folds over it, is met. Two rows are not met to the letter, both already raised and closed by a reason, which I re-tested. POWER-3 says the power paths run on F.Cu end to end: +5V runs 32.4 mm on B.Cu to R1 (finding 2.2, closed as overridden by POWER-2). The board is as it was: the +5V pour, J2 pin 2's +3V3 connection and the /+3V3_REG and +3V3 pours close F.Cu between the +5V parts and R1 except by a route round the top edge past C2 and down the digital region of well over 50 mm, longer than the 32.4 mm on B.Cu and cutting the F.Cu ground pour, so POWER-2's short direct path still overrides the row and the reason still applies. DECOUPLE-2 says C1's and C2's ground sides are at U1 pin 1: they are 5.4 mm and 8.0 mm from it (finding 2.3, closed as overridden by DECOUPLE-2); C1, C2 and C3 each still have two vias of their own beside the ground pad and the F.Cu pour joining the pad to U1 pin 1 and the planes, so DECOUPLE-2 is met by the other way it allows and the reason still applies. Neither is raised again. EMI-3's row says each filter capacitor sits within 3 mm of its connector pin: C5's and C4's supply pads are 2.6 mm edge to edge from J2's pin pads and 3.9 mm centre to centre; I read the row's within 3 mm as the edge-to-edge gap it was judged by in rounds 1 and 2, and met.

**Free pass.** I looked at every tile of every view: both tiles of each of the 16 layer views (the four copper layers, paste, silkscreen, mask, Cmts.User, Edge.Cuts, courtyard and fabrication layers of both sides), of the assembly, class and five net views and of the placement view, both crops and the three 3D views, 55 images in all, and enlarged nothing (no new render). I could not run KiCad's own rule check, `kicad-cli` not being installed on this machine, and rely on the pre-order check's report, which runs it. What I saw beyond the findings below and judged not a defect: the silkscreen, paste, mask, Edge.Cuts, Cmts.User, courtyard and fabrication views each show a filled disc at every through hole and mounting hole and at no surface-mount pad, the renderer drawing the drills; the F.Mask and B.Mask tiles show no opening at any via, so the vias are tented; on both B.Cu tiles the +5V run at x 118.66 and the rings of JP2's pins leave a keyhole opening in the B.Cu ground pour, 0.55 mm wide, which sits within every clearance with the pour running on both sides of the track; the fabrication-layer designators of JP1, JP2 and J3 lie over their own pads and J1, J2 and the others read bottom to top on `layer-F.Fab-r1c1.png` and `layer-F.Fab-r2c1.png`, a layer that is not built; the 3D views draw J1, J2, J3, JP1 and JP2 as top-entry parts with cables leaving upward, and the mounting holes and test points have no model by design. The new PA1_HDR route from U2 pin 12 east along y 114.775 to (134.225, 114.775) and at 45 degrees to J3 pin 1 runs clear of LED_K, which leaves pin 11 for D1 and diverges from it. Every track in the layer views runs along the outline or at 45 degrees to it.

The silkscreen reading direction and overlap, which no item asks about beyond MFG-7 and MFG-8: on `layer-F.SilkS-r1c1.png` and `layer-F.SilkS-r2c1.png` every designator now reads left to right (angle 0, U1's upright), on the two `layer-B.SilkS` tiles TP1 to TP4 are mirrored as a back layer is drawn and on `3d-bottom.png` read left to right upright, so MFG-8 is met. No designator overlaps another designator or a pad, and the designators of J1, JP1, JP2, J3, U2, C1, C2, C3, C4, D1, R1 and H1 to H3 each stand beside their own part and read as its. Two do not name their part on the built board. The designator J2, at (102.25, 116.03), lies inside J2's own body and courtyard (body x 100.6 to 106.4, y 110.5 to 120.5), under the housing that stands above it: on `3d-top.png` J2 is the only connector with no name showing, where J1's stands clear of its housing at (107.55, 104.75) (finding 3.1). The designator C5, at (110.95, 114.3), stands 2.60 mm from C5's centre and 2.55 mm from C4's, touching the corner of each part's courtyard on `assembly-top-r1c1.png` and `layer-F.CrtYd-r1c1.png`, with C4's own designator below C4 at (109.6, 117.06), so a reader of the built board cannot tell whether the name between the two capacitors is C5's or C4's (finding 3.2). Round 2's free pass saw the C5 designator and read it as C5's from the pattern of the others; it is raised here because the pattern is the only thing that tells which, and a built board gives the reader no pattern. I looked for the same two faults in every other designator, by their positions in the board file against the views, and found none.

### Plan and free pass

**Regions.** The floorplan's two regions and their parts are what the layout holds. The low voltage region is (100, 100) to (119, 130) and the digital region (119, 100) to (140, 130); the board carries one rule area for each, on F.Cu, B.Cu, In1.Cu and In2.Cu, at exactly those bounds (every layer view draws them as dashed hatched outlines, and the board file's two zones carry the two polygons), and none for the mains and analog sensing regions, which the plan records as none. `measures.txt` lists the same parts per region as the plan, with OUTSIDE 0 (LENGTH 144.1293 mm, CROSSINGS 11); on `layer-F.CrtYd-r1c1.png` and `layer-F.CrtYd-r2c1.png` every courtyard lies inside its region, JP2's to x 118.77 and C3's from x 119.22 being the nearest to the line. `layout-plan.md` holds no board's own items (one row, none), so there is nothing further to judge there.

**Connectors.** The edges are met: J1 and J2 stand at the left edge (bodies from x 100.6) and J3 at the right edge (body to x 138.3); JP1 and JP2 are link positions with no edge. Pin 1 positions against the table, read from the board file and the assembly tiles: J1 (104, 103.5) against (103, 104), 1.12 mm; J2 (104, 113) against (103, 114), 1.41 mm; J3 (137, 112) against (137, 112), 0.00 mm; JP1 (113, 103.5) against (110, 104), 3.04 mm; JP2 (117, 114.5) against (113, 122), 8.50 mm. FLOOR-5's 2.54 mm leaves J1, J2 and J3 met and JP1 and JP2 departures, both already raised and closed by a reason, which I re-tested against the board as it now stands rather than took on trust. JP1 (finding 1.7, closed by TEST-1 and placement candidate 5): with TP1 where the board has it, at (109, 105), a JP1 anywhere within 2.54 mm of the table position has its body at most 1.77 mm from TP1's pad (pin 1 at (112.54, 104), body from x 111.27, against the pad's edge at x 109.5), short of the 2.0 mm TEST-1 asks of a part taller than 2.6 mm, and at the table position itself TP1's pad would lie inside JP1's body; the plan's placement record names candidate 5 as the one that moved JP1 for that reason, so the closing reason still applies. I tested it with TP1 fixed, as rounds 2 and 3 did; the plan gives no table position for a test point, and I did not take a moved TP1 as part of the board as it stands. JP2 (finding 2.1, closed by POWER-2 and FLOOR-3): /+3V3_REG runs U1 pin 2 to JP2 pin 1 and +3V3 JP2 pin 2 to U2 pin 9, about 12 mm of supply path in the two pours, and the nearest JP2 can stand to its table position while keeping within 2.54 mm of it, pin 1 near (114.4, 120.2), makes that path about 24 mm, twice the length, against POWER-2's short and direct path for a part the plan's POWER-2 and FLOOR-3 rows put in series between U1 and U2, so that reason also still applies. Neither is raised again.

**The yes rows of the plan table.** FLOOR-1, FLOOR-3, FLOOR-4, POWER-1, POWER-2, GROUND-1, GROUND-2, DECOUPLE-1, TEST-1, TEST-2, MFG-1, MFG-4, MFG-5, MFG-6, EMI-1, EMI-2, EMI-3, EMI-5, CONN-3, CONN-4 and WORK-1 to WORK-10 are met as their rows say, the numbers each row gives measured against the board file and the fabrication outlines (U1 12.0 mm from J1 pin 1 and U2 9.8 mm from U1, C3 2.9 mm and C2 3.0 mm from their pins, TP1 to TP4 at least 2.15 mm from the taller parts and 2.5 mm or more from each other, R1 6.0 mm and D1 3.4 mm from U2 pin 11, H1 (104, 126.5), H2 (137, 102.5), H3 (137, 127.5) against the row's near (103.5, 126), (137, 102.5), (137, 127.5), the three vias at J1 pin 2 and the three at J2 pin 3 for the 0.5 A return). The CONN-3 row's J1 and J2 about 25 mm from U2 is 23.6 mm and 21.8 mm centre to centre and 17 mm body to body; the row says about, and what it serves, a cable that leaves away from U2 and never folds over it, is met. Two rows are not met to the letter, both already raised and closed by a reason, which I re-tested. POWER-3 says the power paths run on F.Cu end to end: +5V runs 32.4 mm on B.Cu to R1 (finding 2.2, closed as overridden by POWER-2). The F.Cu is still closed between the +5V parts and R1: the +3V3_REG and +3V3 pours and J2 pin 2's +3V3 connection wall it off westwards, and a route round the top edge and down east of U2 must cross the PA1_HDR track at y 114.775 or the /LED_K track at U2 pin 11, so a layer change is needed in any case and the 5 mA branch takes B.Cu with one via at each end; the reason still applies. DECOUPLE-2 says C1's and C2's ground sides are at U1 pin 1: they are 5.4 mm and 8.0 mm from it (finding 2.3, closed as overridden by DECOUPLE-2); C1 and C2 each still have two vias of their own beside the ground pad and the F.Cu pour joining the pad to U1 pin 1 and the planes, so DECOUPLE-2 is met by the other way it allows and the reason still applies. Neither is raised again. EMI-3's row says each filter capacitor sits within 3 mm of its connector pin: C5's and C4's supply pads are 2.5 mm edge to edge from J2's pin pads and 3.9 mm centre to centre; I read the row's within 3 mm as the edge-to-edge gap it was judged by in rounds 1 to 3, and met.

**Free pass.** I looked at every tile of every view: both tiles of each of the 16 layer views (the four copper layers, paste, silkscreen, mask, Cmts.User, Edge.Cuts, courtyard and fabrication layers of both sides), of the assembly, class and five net views and of the placement view, both crops and the three 3D views, 55 images in all, and cut enlargements of parts of the assembly-top tiles to read the silkscreen against the courtyards (crops of rendered images, no new render). I read coordinates, pad and via nets and courtyard extents from the board file. I could not run KiCad's own rule check, `kicad-cli` not being installed on this machine, and rely on the pre-order check's report, which runs it. What I saw beyond the findings below and judged not a defect: the silkscreen, paste, mask, Edge.Cuts, Cmts.User, courtyard and fabrication views each show a filled disc at every through hole and mounting hole and at no surface-mount pad, the renderer drawing the drills; the F.Mask and B.Mask tiles show no opening at any via, so the vias are tented; ground vias do not show on the layer and net-GND tiles, being flush in the same-coloured pour or plane, so I read their places from the board file; on both B.Cu tiles the +5V run at x 118.66 and the rings of JP2's pins leave a keyhole opening in the B.Cu ground pour, 0.55 mm wide, within every clearance with the pour running on both sides of the track; the fabrication-layer designators of JP1, JP2 and J3 lie over their own pads and J1, J2 and the others read bottom to top on `layer-F.Fab-r1c1.png` and `layer-F.Fab-r2c1.png`, a layer that is not built; the 3D views draw J1, J2, J3, JP1 and JP2 as top-entry parts with cables leaving upward, and the mounting holes and test points have no model by design; the placement view draws the front designator R1 and the back designator TP4 over each other near (134, 121), they being on opposite sides. The PA1_HDR route from U2 pin 12 east along y 114.775 and at 45 degrees to J3 runs clear of /LED_K. Every track in the layer views runs along the outline or at 45 degrees to it. U2's pin 7 ground reaches its via by the 1.1 mm track, as GROUND-4 notes.

The silkscreen reading direction and overlap, which no item asks about beyond MFG-7, MFG-8 and MFG-9: on `layer-F.SilkS-r1c1.png` and `layer-F.SilkS-r2c1.png` every designator reads left to right (angle 0, U1's upright), on the two `layer-B.SilkS` tiles TP1 to TP4 are mirrored as a back layer is drawn and on `3d-bottom.png` read left to right upright, so MFG-8 is met. No designator overlaps another designator or a pad. Which part each names, by its place against the courtyards on `assembly-top-r1c1.png` and `assembly-top-r2c1.png` and the board file: J1's stands right of J1 at (107.55, 104.75), JP1's above JP1, C2's right of C2, U1's right of U1 beside its pin 1 mark, U2's above U2, J3's above J3, C1's left of C1, C5's above C5 (0.22 mm from C5's courtyard, 0.79 mm from C1's, stacked 1.6 mm below C1's name), JP2's above JP2, C3's below C3, C4's below C4, D1's and R1's right of their parts, J2's below J2 (0.25 mm clear of its courtyard, so the housing no longer stands over it and finding 3.1's fault is gone), with H1's name 1.5 mm below it and just above H1's courtyard line, nearer H1 than J2, and TP1 to TP4 beside and below their pads on the back (finding 3.2's fault is gone for C5, whose name now stands nearer C5 than any other part). The strokes of U1's, JP2's and C3's designators graze their own courtyard lines, by about 0.16, 0.11 and 0.06 mm on the enlargements, without reaching a body or a pad; I read MFG-9's no designator inside a courtyard as asking whether a name stands where a part covers it, and did not count a stroke on a courtyard line. The one designator that does stand inside a courtyard is H2's: its name is 1.8 mm from the hole's centre, inside the 2.45 mm courtyard circle that the M2 hole's screw head is drawn to (the ring on `layer-Cmts.User-r1c1.png`), where H1's and H3's, 3.15 mm from theirs, stand outside theirs (finding 4.1).

### Plan and free pass

**Regions.** The floorplan's two regions and their parts are what the layout holds. The low voltage region is (100, 100) to (119, 130) and the digital region (119, 100) to (140, 130); the board carries one rule area for each, on F.Cu, B.Cu, In1.Cu and In2.Cu, at exactly those bounds (every layer view draws them as dashed hatched outlines, the placement view names both regions, and the board file's two keep-out zones carry the two polygons), and none for the mains and analog sensing regions, which the plan records as none. `measures.txt` lists the same parts per region as the plan, with OUTSIDE 0 (LENGTH 144.1293 mm, CROSSINGS 11); on `layer-F.CrtYd-r1c1.png` and `layer-F.CrtYd-r2c1.png` every courtyard lies inside its region, JP2's to x 118.77, C2's to x 118.15 and C3's from x 119.22 being the nearest to the line. `layout-plan.md` holds no board's own items (one row, none), so there is nothing further to judge there.

**Connectors.** The edges are met: J1 and J2 stand at the left edge (bodies from x 100.6) and J3 at the right edge (body to x 138.3); JP1 and JP2 are link positions with no edge. Pin 1 positions against the table, read from the board file and the assembly tiles: J1 (104, 103.5) against (103, 104), 1.12 mm; J2 (104, 113) against (103, 114), 1.41 mm; J3 (137, 112) against (137, 112), 0.00 mm; JP1 (113, 103.5) against (110, 104), 3.04 mm; JP2 (117, 114.5) against (113, 122), 8.50 mm. FLOOR-5's 2.54 mm leaves J1, J2 and J3 met and JP1 and JP2 departures, both already raised and closed by a reason, which I re-tested against the board as it now stands rather than took on trust. JP1 (finding 1.7, closed by TEST-1 and placement candidate 5): TP1 is at (109, 105) and its pad reaches x 109.5; JP1's body is 2.54 mm wide (x 111.73 to 114.27 at pin 1 x 113); at the table position TP1's pad would lie inside JP1's body, and the furthest JP1 can stand toward the table within 2.54 mm of it, pin 1 at x 112.54, puts the body edge at x 111.27, 1.77 mm from the pad, short of the 2.0 mm TEST-1 asks of a part taller than 2.6 mm; moving it up or down within the tolerance does not help, since the body is 5.1 mm long and the pad lies beside its first half, and the plan's placement record names candidate 5 as the one that moved JP1 for that reason, so the closing reason still applies. JP2 (finding 2.1, closed by POWER-2 and FLOOR-3): /+3V3_REG runs U1 pin 2 to JP2 pin 1 and +3V3 JP2 pin 2 to U2 pin 9, about 12 mm of supply path in the two pours, and the nearest JP2 can stand to its table position while keeping within 2.54 mm of it, pin 1 near (114.4, 120.2), makes that path about 24 mm, twice the length, against POWER-2's short and direct path for a part the plan's POWER-2 and FLOOR-3 rows put in series between U1 and U2, so that reason also still applies. Neither is raised again.

**The yes rows of the plan table.** FLOOR-1, FLOOR-3, FLOOR-4, POWER-1, POWER-2, GROUND-1, GROUND-2, DECOUPLE-1, TEST-1, TEST-2, MFG-1, MFG-4, MFG-5, MFG-6, EMI-1, EMI-2, EMI-3, EMI-5, CONN-3, CONN-4 and WORK-1 to WORK-10 are met as their rows say, the numbers each row gives measured against the board file and the fabrication outlines (U1 12.0 mm from J1 pin 1 and U2 9.8 mm from U1, C3 2.9 mm and C2 3.0 mm from their pins, TP1 to TP4 at least 2.15 mm from the taller parts and 10.9 mm or more from each other, R1 6.0 mm and D1 3.4 mm from U2 pin 11, H1 (104, 126.5), H2 (137, 102.5), H3 (137, 127.5) against the row's near (103.5, 126), (137, 102.5), (137, 127.5), the three vias at J1 pin 2 and the three at J2 pin 3 for the 0.5 A return, two ground vias of their own beside each of C1, C2, C3, C4, C5 and U1 pin 1). DECOUPLE-2 is also a yes row; the plan's GROUND-4 row says no, but the item as amended applies to this board and is judged above. The CONN-3 row's J1 and J2 about 25 mm from U2 is 23.6 mm and 21.8 mm centre to centre and 15.5 mm courtyard to courtyard; the row says about, and what it serves, a cable that leaves away from U2 and never folds over it, is met. Two rows are not met to the letter, both already raised and closed by a reason, which I re-tested. POWER-3 says the power paths run on F.Cu end to end: +5V runs 32.4 mm on B.Cu to R1 (finding 2.2, closed as overridden by POWER-2). The F.Cu is still closed between the +5V parts and R1: the +3V3 pour (x 103.7 to 123.1, y 111.3 to 117.5) and the /+3V3_REG pour wall it off westwards and southwards and J2 pin 2's +3V3 connection is part of the first, and a route round the top edge and down east of U2 must cross the PA1_HDR track at y 114.775 or the /LED_K track at U2 pin 11, so a layer change is needed in any case and the 5 mA branch takes B.Cu with one via at each end; the reason still applies. DECOUPLE-2 says C1's and C2's ground sides are at U1 pin 1: they are 5.4 mm and 8.0 mm from it (finding 2.3, closed as overridden by DECOUPLE-2); C1 and C2 each still have two vias of their own beside the ground pad and the F.Cu pour joining the pad to U1 pin 1 and the planes, so DECOUPLE-2 is met by the other way it allows and the reason still applies. Neither is raised again. EMI-3's row says each filter capacitor sits within 3 mm of its connector pin: C5's and C4's supply pads are 2.5 mm edge to edge from J2's pin pads and 3.9 mm centre to centre; I read the row's within 3 mm as the edge-to-edge gap it was judged by in rounds 1 to 4, and met.

**Free pass.** I looked at every tile of every view: both tiles of each of the 16 layer views (the four copper layers, paste, silkscreen, mask, Cmts.User, Edge.Cuts, courtyard and fabrication layers of both sides), of the assembly, class and five net views and of the placement view, both crops and the three 3D views, 55 images in all, and cut enlargements of the assembly-top tiles and measured stroke and courtyard-line positions on the rendered pixels to read the silkscreen against the courtyards (crops of rendered images, no new render). I read coordinates, pad and via nets and courtyard extents from the board file, and checked the board-wide figures of `method-proof.kicad_pro` and the rules in `method-proof.kicad_dru` against the rows of `design-rules.md`. I did not run KiCad's own rule check and rely on the pre-order check's report, which runs it. What I saw beyond the findings below and judged not a defect: the silkscreen, paste, mask, Edge.Cuts, Cmts.User, courtyard and fabrication views each show a filled disc at every through hole and mounting hole and at no surface-mount pad, the renderer drawing the drills; the F.Mask and B.Mask tiles show no opening at any via, so the vias are tented, and the designators C5, D1, JP2 and TP3 stand over tented ground or tap vias with no exposed copper under them, which the silkscreen clearance check does not count; ground vias do not show on the layer and net-GND tiles, being flush in the same-coloured pour or plane, so I read their places from the board file; the B.Cu tiles show keyhole openings in the pour at the TP stubs and round JP2's pin rings, within every clearance with pour on both sides of the track; the fabrication-layer designators of JP1, JP2 and J3 lie over their own pads and J1, J2 and the others read bottom to top on `layer-F.Fab-r1c1.png` and `layer-F.Fab-r2c1.png`, a layer that is not built; the 3D views draw J1, J2, J3, JP1 and JP2 as top-entry parts with cables leaving upward, and the mounting holes and test points have no model by design; the placement view draws the front designator R1 and the back designator TP4 over each other near (134, 121), they being on opposite sides, and on `3d-angled.png` the names J2 and H1 touch at the angle of the view, though 0.35 mm apart on the board. The PA1_HDR route from U2 pin 12 east along y 114.775 and at 45 degrees to J3 runs clear of /LED_K. Every track in the layer views runs along the outline or at 45 degrees to it. U2's pin 7 ground reaches its via by the 1.1 mm track, as GROUND-4 notes.

The silkscreen reading direction and overlap, which no item asks about beyond MFG-7, MFG-8 and MFG-9: on `layer-F.SilkS-r1c1.png` and `layer-F.SilkS-r2c1.png` every designator reads left to right (angle 0 in the board file, U1's upright), on the two `layer-B.SilkS` tiles TP1 to TP4 are mirrored as a back layer is drawn and on `3d-bottom.png` read left to right upright, so MFG-8 is met. No designator overlaps another designator or a pad. Which part each names, by its place against the courtyards on `assembly-top-r1c1.png` and `assembly-top-r2c1.png`, with the stroke and line positions measured on the pixels and the glyph extents estimated from the font: J1's stands right of J1 at (107.55, 104.75), the stroke of its J grazing J1's courtyard line by 0.06 mm, as round 4 read such grazes (not counted: no body stands over the name); JP1's above JP1 (0.09 mm clear); C2's right of C2 (0.09 mm); U1's right of U1 and 0.1 mm outside its courtyard, beside its pin 1 mark; U2's above U2; J3's above J3; C1's left of C1 (0.5 mm), with C5's above C5 (0.32 mm, against 0.8 mm from C1's courtyard); C3's below C3 (0.25 mm, against 0.65 mm from U2's); C4's below C4; D1's and R1's right of their parts; J2's below J2 (0.4 mm), H1's name 1.6 mm below it and 0.15 mm above H1's courtyard, nearer H1; H3's above H3; H2's now left of H2 at (133, 102.5), 0.7 mm outside its courtyard circle, so finding 4.1's fault is gone; TP1 to TP4 beside and below their pads on the back (about 0.36 mm). The one that does not name its part is JP2's, at (117, 112): it stands in the 1.53 mm gap between U1's courtyard bottom line (y 111.19) and JP2's top line (y 112.72), its glyphs spanning y 111.40 to 112.51, 0.21 mm from each, and 0.30 mm from U1's silkscreen outline against 0.57 mm from JP2's, so on `assembly-top-r1c1.png`, `layer-F.SilkS-r1c1.png` and `3d-top.png` it hangs between the regulator and the link and a reader of the built board cannot tell which it names (finding 5.1). Round 4 recorded its strokes grazing JP2's courtyard line by 0.11 mm, and finding 4.1's closure moved it clear of that line, to here.

### Plan and free pass

**Regions.** The floorplan's two regions and their parts are what the layout holds. The low voltage region is (100, 100) to (119, 130) and the digital region (119, 100) to (140, 130); the board file carries one rule area for each, with no restriction set, on F.Cu, B.Cu, In1.Cu and In2.Cu at exactly those bounds (every layer view draws them as dashed hatched outlines and the placement view names both regions), and none for the mains and analog sensing regions, which the plan records as none. `measures.txt` lists the same parts per region as the plan, with OUTSIDE 0 (LENGTH 144.1293 mm, CROSSINGS 11); on `layer-F.CrtYd-r1c1.png` and `layer-F.CrtYd-r2c1.png` every courtyard lies inside its region, JP2's to x 118.77, C2's to x 118.15, U1's to x 117.93 and C3's from x 119.22 being the nearest to the line. `layout-plan.md` holds no board's own items (one row, none), so there is nothing further to judge there.

**Connectors.** The edges are met: J1 and J2 stand at the left edge (courtyards from x 100.1) and J3 at the right edge (courtyard to x 138.77); JP1 and JP2 are link positions with no edge. Pin 1 positions against the table, read from the board file and checked on the assembly tiles: J1 (104, 103.5) against (103, 104), 1.12 mm; J2 (104, 113) against (103, 114), 1.41 mm; J3 (137, 112) against (137, 112), 0.00 mm; JP1 (113, 103.5) against (110, 104), 3.04 mm; JP2 (117, 114.5) against (113, 122), 8.50 mm. FLOOR-5's 2.54 mm leaves J1, J2 and J3 met and JP1 and JP2 departures, both already raised and closed by a reason, which I re-tested against the board as it now stands rather than took on trust. JP1 (finding 1.7, closed by TEST-1 and placement candidate 5): TP1 is at (109, 105), its pad x 108.5 to 109.5, y 104.5 to 105.5; JP1's body is 2.54 mm wide and 5.08 mm long (x 111.73 to 114.27, y 102.23 to 107.31 at pin 1 (113, 103.5)); at the table position the body would cover TP1's pad, and the furthest toward the table JP1 can stand within 2.54 mm of it, pin 1 at x 112.54, puts the body edge at x 111.27, 1.77 mm from the pad, short of the 2.0 mm TEST-1 asks of a part taller than 2.6 mm; moving it up or down inside the tolerance does not help, the body being 5.08 mm long and the pad beside its first half; the plan's placement record names candidate 5 as the one that moved JP1 for that reason, so the closing reason still applies. I tested it with TP1 where the board has it, as rounds 2 to 5 did, the plan giving no position for a test point. JP2 (finding 2.1, closed by POWER-2 and FLOOR-3): /+3V3_REG runs U1 pin 2 to JP2 pin 1 and +3V3 JP2 pin 2 to U2 pin 9, about 12.3 mm of supply path in the two pours outside the link, and the nearest JP2 can stand to its table position while keeping within 2.54 mm of it, pin 1 near (114.2, 119.8), makes that path about 23.1 mm, nearly twice as long, against POWER-2's short and direct path and FLOOR-3's adjacency for a part the plan's POWER-2 row puts in series between U1 and U2, so that reason also still applies. Neither is raised again.

**The yes rows of the plan table.** FLOOR-1, FLOOR-3, FLOOR-4, POWER-1, POWER-2, GROUND-1, GROUND-2, DECOUPLE-1, TEST-1, TEST-2, MFG-1, MFG-4, MFG-5, MFG-6, EMI-1, EMI-2, EMI-5 and WORK-1 to WORK-10 are met as their rows say, the numbers each row gives measured against the board file and the fabrication outlines (U1 13.4 mm from J1 pin 1 and 10.2 mm from U2, C3's pad 2.9 mm from U2 pin 9 and C2's 3.0 mm from U1 pin 2, C1's pad 1.1 mm from U1's input pad, TP1 to TP4 at least 2.15 mm from the taller parts and 11.0 mm or more from each other, R1 6.0 mm and D1 3.4 mm from U2 pin 11, H1 (104, 126.5), H2 (137, 102.5), H3 (137, 127.5) against the row's near (103.5, 126), (137, 102.5), (137, 127.5), the three vias at J1 pin 2 and the three at J2 pin 3 for the 0.5 A return, the PA1_HDR route 9.49 mm against an 8.78 mm straight tree). DECOUPLE-2 and EMI-3, also yes rows, are met in the sense the items give, with the clauses below. The CONN-3 row's J1 and J2 about 25 mm from U2 is 23.6 mm and 21.8 mm centre to centre and 15.1 mm courtyard to courtyard; the row says about, and what it serves, a cable that leaves away from U2 and never folds over it, is met. Three clauses are not met to the letter, each already raised and closed by a reason, which I re-tested against the board as it stands. POWER-3's row says the power paths run on F.Cu end to end: +5V runs 32.4 mm on B.Cu to R1 (finding 2.2, closed as overridden by POWER-2). F.Cu is still closed between the +5V parts and R1: the +3V3 pour (x 103.7 to 123.1, y 111.3 to 117.5 in the board file, J2 pin 2's connection part of it) and the /+3V3_REG pour (x 116.2 to 118.8, y 105.2 to 114.9) wall it off to the east and south, JP1 and C2 leave no gap between them, a route north of C2 would first have to cross the /+5V_IN track at y 103.5, and one round the east of J3 would first have to cross a pour or a track too, so a layer change is needed in any case and the 5 mA branch takes B.Cu with one via at each end; the reason still applies. DECOUPLE-2's row says C1's and C2's ground sides are at U1 pin 1: they are 5.4 mm and 8.0 mm from it (finding 2.3, closed as overridden by DECOUPLE-2); C1 and C2 each still have two vias of their own beside the ground pad and the F.Cu pour joining the pad to U1 pin 1 and the planes, so DECOUPLE-2 is met by the other way it allows and the reason still applies. EMI-3's row says each filter capacitor sits within 3 mm of its connector pin: C5's and C4's supply pads are 2.5 mm edge to edge from J2's pin pads and 3.9 mm centre to centre; I read the row's within 3 mm as the edge-to-edge gap it was judged by in rounds 1 to 5, and met. Neither of the first two is raised again. The plan's GROUND-4 row says no, but U1 and U2 each have a ground pin and the item as it now stands applies; it is judged above and passes. The plan table has no rows for FLOOR-5, GROUND-5, DECOUPLE-6 and MFG-7 to MFG-9, which were added after the baseline; each is judged above on its own item.

**Free pass.** I looked at every tile of every view: both tiles of each of the 32 layer views (the four copper layers, paste, silkscreen, mask, Cmts.User, Edge.Cuts, courtyard and fabrication layers of both sides), of the two assembly views, the class map, the five net views and the placement view, both crops and the three 3D views, 55 images in all. Beyond the verdicts I read coordinates, pad and via nets and courtyard extents from the board file, checked every track segment of the file for direction and layer, checked `method-proof.kicad_pro` and `method-proof.kicad_dru` against the rows of `design-rules.md`, and measured the glyph extents of the silkscreen names on the pixels of the rendered tiles. I did not run KiCad's own rule check and rely on the pre-order check's report, which runs it. What I saw beyond the plan comparison and judged not a defect: the silkscreen, paste, mask, Edge.Cuts, Cmts.User, courtyard and fabrication views each show a filled disc at every through hole and mounting hole and at no surface-mount pad, the renderer drawing the drills; the F.Mask and B.Mask tiles show no opening at any via, so the vias are tented, and the designators D1 and C5 and the back name TP3 stand over tented vias with no exposed copper under them; ground vias do not show on the layer and net-GND tiles, being flush in the same-coloured pour or plane, so I read their places from the board file; the B.Cu tiles show keyhole openings in the pour at the four test point stubs and round JP2's pin rings, where the +5V run at x 118.66 passes 0.56 mm from JP2 pin 2's pad, within every clearance with pour on both sides; the fabrication-layer designators of JP1, JP2 and J3 lie over their own pads and J1, J2 and the others read bottom to top on `layer-F.Fab-r1c1.png` and `layer-F.Fab-r2c1.png`, a layer that is not built; the 3D views draw J1, J2, J3, JP1 and JP2 as top-entry parts with cables leaving upward, and the mounting holes and test points have no model by design; the placement view draws the front designator R1 and the back designator TP4 over each other near (134, 121), they being on opposite sides, and on `3d-angled.png` the names J2 and H1 touch at the angle of the view, though 0.33 mm apart on the board. The JST XH pads of J1 and J2 are 1.7 mm by 2.0 and 1.95 mm, longer across the pin row than along it; the GROUND-5 row speaks of the board's widest pad as 1.7 mm across, which holds along the row where the rings can meet, and the tiles show copper between the rings (see the GROUND-5 verdict). The /+3V3_REG pour's minimum thickness is 0.09 mm in the board file where the other pours have 0.2 mm; every part of that pour is at least 1.1 mm wide on the tiles, so the setting changes nothing seen and breaks no rule. The R1 feed on B.Cu runs along x 118.66, its centre 0.34 mm inside the low voltage region's edge, and crosses no other net's copper. The PA1_HDR route from U2 pin 12 east along y 114.775 and at 45 degrees to J3 runs clear of /LED_K. Every track in the layer views runs along the outline or at 45 degrees to it.

The silkscreen reading direction and overlap, which no item asks about beyond MFG-7 to MFG-9: on `layer-F.SilkS-r1c1.png`, `layer-F.SilkS-r2c1.png` and `3d-top.png` every designator reads left to right (angle 0 in the board file, U1's upright), on the two `layer-B.SilkS` tiles TP1 to TP4 are mirrored as a back layer is drawn and on `3d-bottom.png` read left to right upright, so MFG-8 is met. No designator overlaps another designator, a pad or a part outline: the closest pairs are C1's and C5's names (0.5 mm apart, stacked) and J2's and H1's (0.33 mm), and the nearest name to a part outline is C2's, 0.18 mm from C2's silkscreen U. Which part each names, by its place against the courtyards on `assembly-top-r1c1.png` and `assembly-top-r2c1.png`, with the glyph extents measured on the pixels and the courtyards read from the board file: J1's stands right of J1 at x 106.78 to 108.25, the hook of its J within 0.07 mm of J1's courtyard line (x 106.85) and 0.5 mm from the housing (x 106.35), which does not cover it, so I do not count it as inside; JP1's above JP1 (0.11 mm); C2's right of C2 (0.09 mm); U1's right of U1 (0.13 mm); U2's above U2 (0.21 mm); J3's above J3 (0.12 mm); C1's left of C1 (0.54 mm), with C5's above C5 (0.36 mm, against 0.81 mm from C1's courtyard); C3's below C3 (0.19 mm, against 0.59 mm from U2's); C4's below C4 (0.15 mm); D1's and R1's right of their parts (0.26 mm); J2's below J2 (0.35 mm), H1's name 1.8 mm below J2's courtyard and 0.2 mm above H1's courtyard line, nearer H1; H3's above H3 (0.21 mm); H2's left of H2, 0.71 mm outside its courtyard circle (finding 4.1's fault gone); TP1 to TP4 below their pads on the back (0.25 to 0.3 mm). JP2's name, the one that did not name its part in round 5, now stands below JP2 at (117, 119.6), its glyphs x 115.72 to 118.21 and y 118.99 to 120.10, 0.17 mm below JP2's courtyard bottom line (y 118.82) and 2.8 mm from the nearest other part's courtyard, C3's; on `assembly-top-r2c1.png`, `layer-F.SilkS-r2c1.png` and `3d-top.png` it reads as JP2's and nothing hangs between U1 and the link, so finding 5.1's fault is gone. I looked for the same two faults, a name inside a courtyard and a name nearer another part, in every other designator and found none.

Every earlier finding is closed: 1.1 to 1.4, 1.6, 1.8, 2.4, 3.1, 3.2, 4.1 and 5.1 by changes to the board that this round judges met (the plated hole spacing rule, the +5V tap moved clear of C1 and U1, the second vias beside each capacitor and U1 pin 1, EMI-3's two vias each, J3's move to its table position, and the designators' reading direction, places and silkscreen layer), and 1.5, 1.7, 2.1, 2.2 and 2.3 by reasons that I re-tested above against the board as it stands and that still apply. The board judged here is the one the closing change of finding 5.1 produced, and this round raises nothing against it.

### Findings

This round raises no finding.

### Plan and free pass

**Regions.** The floorplan's two regions and their parts are what the layout holds. The low voltage region is (100, 100) to (119, 130) and the digital region (119, 100) to (140, 130); the board carries one rule area for each, on F.Cu, B.Cu, In1.Cu and In2.Cu at exactly those bounds with no restriction (every layer tile draws them as dashed hatched outlines and the placement tiles name both regions), and none for the mains and analog sensing regions, which the plan records as none. `measures.txt` lists the same parts per region as the plan, with OUTSIDE 0; on `layer-F.CrtYd-r1c1.png`, `layer-F.CrtYd-r2c1.png`, `layer-B.CrtYd-r1c1.png` and `layer-B.CrtYd-r2c1.png` every courtyard lies inside its region, JP2's to x 118.77, C2's to x 118.15, U1's to x 117.93 and C3's from x 119.22 being the nearest to the line. `layout-plan.md` holds no board's own items (one row, none), so there is nothing further to judge there.

**Connectors.** The edges are met: J1 and J2 stand at the left edge (courtyards from x 100.1) and J3 at the right edge (courtyard to x 138.77); JP1 and JP2 are link positions with no edge. Pin 1 positions against the table, read from the board file and checked on the assembly tiles: J1 (104, 103.5) against (103, 104), 1.12 mm; J2 (104, 113) against (103, 114), 1.41 mm; J3 (137, 112) against (137, 112), 0.00 mm; JP1 (113, 103.5) against (110, 104), 3.04 mm; JP2 (117, 114.5) against (113, 122), 8.50 mm. FLOOR-5's 2.54 mm leaves J1, J2 and J3 met and JP1 and JP2 departures, both already raised and closed by a reason (findings 1.7 and 2.1), which I re-tested against the board as it now stands. JP1 (finding 1.7, TEST-1 and placement candidate 5): TP1 is at (109, 105), its pad x 108.5 to 109.5; JP1's body is 2.54 mm wide and 5.08 mm long (x 111.73 to 114.27, y 102.23 to 107.31 at pin 1 (113, 103.5)); at the table position the body would cover TP1's pad, and the furthest toward the table JP1 can stand within 2.54 mm of it, pin 1 at x 112.54, puts the body edge at x 111.27, 1.77 mm from the pad, short of the 2.0 mm TEST-1 asks of a part taller than 2.6 mm; moving it up or down inside the tolerance does not help, the pad lying beside the body's first half. The plan gives no position for a test point and I tested with TP1 where the board has it, as the earlier rounds did; the plan's placement record names candidate 5 as the one that moved JP1 for this reason. The reason still applies. JP2 (finding 2.1, POWER-2 and FLOOR-3): /+3V3_REG runs U1 pin 2 to JP2 pin 1 and +3V3 JP2 pin 2 to U2 pin 9, about 12.3 mm of supply path outside the link, and the nearest JP2 can stand to its table position within 2.54 mm of it, pin 1 near (114.2, 119.8), makes that path about 23 mm, nearly twice as long, against POWER-2's short and direct path and FLOOR-3's adjacency for a part the plan's POWER-2 row puts in series between U1 and U2. The reason still applies. Neither is raised again.

**The yes rows of the plan table.** FLOOR-1, FLOOR-3, FLOOR-4, POWER-1, POWER-2, GROUND-1, GROUND-2, DECOUPLE-1, TEST-1, TEST-2, MFG-1, MFG-4, MFG-5, MFG-6, EMI-1, EMI-2, EMI-5 and WORK-1 to WORK-10 are met as their rows say, the numbers each row gives measured against the board file and the fabrication outlines (U1 13.4 mm from J1 pin 1 and 10.2 mm from U2, C3's pad centre 2.9 mm from U2 pin 9, TP1 to TP4 at least 2.1 mm from the taller parts and 11.0 mm or more from each other, R1 6.0 mm and D1 3.4 mm from U2 pin 11, H1 (104, 126.5), H2 (137, 102.5), H3 (137, 127.5) against the row's near (103.5, 126), (137, 102.5), (137, 127.5), the three vias at J1 pin 2 and the three at J2 pin 3 for the 0.5 A return, the PA1_HDR route 9.49 mm against an 8.78 mm straight tree). DECOUPLE-2 and EMI-3, also yes rows, are met in the sense the items give, with the clauses below. The CONN-3 row's J1 and J2 about 25 mm from U2 is 23.6 mm and 21.8 mm centre to centre and 15.1 mm courtyard to courtyard; the row says about, and what it serves, a cable that leaves away from U2, is met. Three clauses are not met to the letter, each already raised and closed by a reason, which I re-tested against the board as it stands. POWER-3's row says the power paths run on F.Cu end to end: +5V runs 32.4 mm on B.Cu to R1 (finding 2.2, closed as overridden by POWER-2). F.Cu is still closed between the +5V parts and R1: the +3V3 pour (about x 103.7 to 123.1, y 111.3 to 117.5) and the /+3V3_REG pour (about x 116.2 to 118.8, y 101 to 114.9) wall it off to the east and south, JP1 and C2 leave no gap between them, and the north edge leaves no room above C2, so a layer change is needed in any case and the 5 mA branch takes B.Cu with one via at each end; the reason still applies. DECOUPLE-2's row says C1's and C2's ground sides are at U1 pin 1: they are 5.4 mm and 8.0 mm from it (finding 2.3, closed as overridden by DECOUPLE-2); C1 and C2 each still have two vias of their own beside the ground pad and the F.Cu pour joining the pad to U1 pin 1 and the planes, so DECOUPLE-2 is met by the other way it allows and the reason still applies. EMI-3's row says each filter capacitor sits within 3 mm of its connector pin: C5's and C4's supply pads are 2.5 mm edge to edge from J2's pin pads and 3.9 mm centre to centre. The method does not say which way a plan row's within 3 mm is measured; I read it edge to edge, as rounds 1 to 6 did, and met, and record that read by centre to centre it would be 0.9 mm over. Of the plan's other rows I checked DECOUPLE-1's C3 within 3 mm (2.88 mm pad centre to pin centre) and WORK-10's 1.5 by 0.4 mm leads (1.475 by 0.4 in the file). The plan's GROUND-4 row says no, but U1 and U2 each have a ground pin and the item as it stands applies; it is judged above and passes. The plan table has no rows for FLOOR-5, GROUND-5, DECOUPLE-6 and MFG-7 to MFG-9, which were added after the baseline; each is judged above on its own item. The placement view's final measures, LENGTH 144.1293 mm and CROSSINGS 11, are above those the plan recorded for the chosen candidate 5 (141.0259 mm and 9); the plan's yes row for WORK-1 asks that each connection be short and none cross another on F.Cu, which the copper meets, and the 11 crossings are the straight-line trees of the four pour-carried nets (GND 8, +5V 6, +3V3 4, /+3V3_REG 4 in `measures.txt`, each crossing counted for both nets), the signal nets having none, so I judged WORK-1 on the views and did not read the rise as a departure.

**Free pass.** I looked at every tile of every view: both tiles of each of the 32 layer views (the four copper layers, paste, silkscreen, mask, Cmts.User, Edge.Cuts, courtyard and fabrication layers of both sides), of the two assembly views, the class map, the five net views and the placement view, both crops and the three 3D views, 55 images in all. Beyond the verdicts I read coordinates, pad and via nets and courtyard extents from the board file, checked the board-wide figures of `method-proof.kicad_pro` and the rules in `method-proof.kicad_dru` against the rows of `design-rules.md`, and recomputed the Plated hole spacing figure from the pads and zones in the board file (the row was re-derived to 1.45 mm since round 6, which judged 1.35 mm; this round's GROUND-5 verdict is on the formula as the item now states it, with the widest annulus J2's 0.375 mm). I did not run KiCad's own rule check and rely on the pre-order check's report, which runs it. What I saw beyond the plan comparison and judged not a defect: the silkscreen, paste, mask, Edge.Cuts, Cmts.User, courtyard and fabrication views each show a filled disc at every through hole and mounting hole and at no surface-mount pad, the renderer drawing the drills; the F.Mask and B.Mask tiles show no opening at any via, so the vias are tented; ground vias do not show on the layer and net-GND tiles, being flush in the same-coloured pour or plane, so I read their places from the board file; the B.Cu tiles show keyhole openings in the pour at the four test point stubs and round JP2's pin rings, where the +5V run at x 118.66 passes 0.56 mm from JP2 pin 2's pad, within every clearance with pour on both sides; the fabrication-layer designators of JP1, JP2 and J3 lie over their own pads on `layer-F.Fab-r1c1.png` and `layer-F.Fab-r2c1.png`, a layer that is not built; the 3D views draw J1, J2, J3, JP1 and JP2 as top-entry parts with cables leaving upward, and the mounting holes and test points have no model by design (the index says so); the placement view draws the front designator R1 and the back designator TP4 over each other near (134, 121), they being on opposite sides, and on `3d-angled.png` the names J2 and H1 touch at the angle of the view, though about 0.33 mm apart on the board; the +5V feed to R1 on B.Cu runs along x 118.66, 0.34 mm inside the low voltage region's edge, and crosses no other net's copper; the /+3V3_REG pour's minimum thickness is 0.09 mm in the board file where the other pours have 0.2 mm, every part of that pour is at least 1.0 mm wide on the tiles, so it changes nothing seen and breaks no rule. EMI-5 and the order of R1 and D1: the series resistor R1 stands 6.0 mm from U2 pin 11 with D1 between it and the pin, where swapping them would put R1 3.4 mm from the pin; I read EMI-5 as met because the plan's row fixes both within 6 mm and the node between D1 and the pin carries TP4, and record that the item says as close as it can be. GROUND-4: U2 pin 7 reaches its via by a short track, judged met above for the reason given there. The silkscreen reading direction and overlap: on `layer-F.SilkS-r1c1.png`, `layer-F.SilkS-r2c1.png` and `3d-top.png` every designator reads left to right, on the two `layer-B.SilkS` tiles TP1 to TP4 are mirrored as a back layer is drawn and on `3d-bottom.png` read left to right upright; no designator overlaps another designator, a pad or a part outline, and each stands beside its own part as the MFG-9 verdict sets out. Every track in the layer views runs along the outline or at 45 degrees to it.

Every earlier finding stands as the earlier rounds closed it: 1.1 to 1.4, 1.6, 1.8, 2.4, 3.1, 3.2, 4.1 and 5.1 by changes to the board that this round judges met, and 1.5, 1.7, 2.1, 2.2 and 2.3 by reasons that I re-tested above against the board as it stands and that still apply. This round raises nothing against the board it judges.

### Findings

This round raises no finding.
